View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_1

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

Sample Input 1

5

ABCBC

ACAAB

Sample Output 1

Yes

AC..B

.BA.C

C.BA.

BA.C.

..CBA


The grid in the output example satisfies all the following conditions, so it will be treated as correct.

- Each row contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- Each column contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost characters written in the rows are A, B, C, B, C from top to bottom.
- The topmost characters written in the columns are A, C, A, A, B from left to right.

Sample Input 2

3

AAA

BBB

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where we need to place exactly one A, one B, and one C in each row and each column.
2. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i].
3. The topmost character in column i must match C[i].
4. We need to determine if such a configuration is possible, and if so, output one valid configuration.

## Key Insights

1. Since each row and column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C, we have constraints on where we can place these characters.
2. The leftmost character constraint means that in each row, the first non-empty cell must match the corresponding character in R.
3. The topmost character constraint means that in each column, the first non-empty cell must match the corresponding character in C.
4. Since we need exactly one of each letter per row/column, we can think of this as a constraint satisfaction problem.

## Approach

1. First, check if the constraints are even possible:
   - For each row, we need to place A, B, C with the leftmost character matching R[i]
   - For each column, we need to place A, B, C with the topmost character matching C[i]

2. The key observation is that if we have a conflict in the first positions, it's impossible. For example, if R[i] = 'A' but C[j] = 'A' for some j, we need to ensure the constraints can be satisfied.

3. We can use backtracking to try different placements, but given the small constraints (N ≤ 5), we can also try a more systematic approach.

4. Another insight: since each row must have exactly one A, B, C, and each column must have exactly one A, B, C, we can think of this as a Latin square variant with additional constraints.

5. We can try to place the characters greedily or systematically, ensuring that the leftmost and topmost constraints are satisfied.

Let me implement a solution:

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Check if it's even possible based on constraints
    # For each row, the leftmost character must match R[i]
    # For each column, the topmost character must match C[i]
    
    # We need to ensure that for each row, we can have the required leftmost character
    # and for each column, we can have the required topmost character
    
    # Try all possible valid configurations using backtracking
    # Since N is small (3-5), we can try a systematic approach
    
    def is_valid(grid):
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None or leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None or topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    # Generate a grid with the constraints
    # We'll try to place A, B, C in positions that satisfy the leftmost and topmost constraints
    
    # We can use backtracking to try different arrangements
    def backtrack(grid, row, col):
        if row == N:
            return grid if is_valid(grid) else None
        
        # Move to next row
        if col == N:
            return backtrack(grid, row + 1, 0)
        
        # If current cell is already filled, move to next cell
        if grid[row][col] != '.':
            return backtrack(grid, row, col + 1)
        
        # Try placing A, B, C or leave empty
        # But we need to be careful about constraints
        
        # First check if we can place a character here
        # We must ensure that the leftmost character in the row will be correct when we place something
        
        # Let's try to fill the grid systematically
        # For each position, try A, B, C, or leave empty
        
        # Try placing A
        grid[row][col] = 'A'
        result = backtrack(grid, row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
        
        # Try placing B
        grid[row][col] = 'B'
        result = backtrack(grid, row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
        
        # Try placing C
        grid[row][col] = 'C'
        result = backtrack(grid, row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
        
        # Try leaving empty
        grid[row][col] = '.'
        result = backtrack(grid, row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
        
        # If no valid configuration found, backtrack
        return None
    
    # Initialize empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # First, we need to ensure the constraints are satisfied
    # The leftmost character constraint means that in row i, the first non-empty cell must be R[i]
    # The topmost character constraint means that in column j, the first non-empty cell must be C[j]
    
    # Let's try to place the characters in a way that satisfies the constraints
    # We can use a more systematic approach: try to place each letter at positions that satisfy the constraints
    
    # For each row, we need to have the leftmost character be R[i]
    # For each column, we need to have the topmost character be C[j]
    
    # We can try to find a configuration by first placing the required characters at the beginning positions
    # and then filling the rest
    
    # Create a solution that tries to satisfy the constraints
    
    # Let's use a different approach: try to find one valid configuration
    # Since N is small, we can try backtracking but with constraints
    
    def can_place(grid, r, c, char):
        # Check if we can place char at (r,c) without violating constraints
        # First, check row constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in row r, it should be R[r]
        row_non_empty = [grid[r][j] for j in range(c) if grid[r][j] != '.']
        if not row_non_empty and char != R[r]:
            return False
            
        # Check column constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in column c, it should be C[c]
        col_non_empty = [grid[i][c] for i in range(r) if grid[i][c] != '.']
        if not col_non_empty and char != C[c]:
            return False
            
        # Check if placing this character will violate the one-per-letter rule
        # We don't check this yet because we'll handle it in the validation function
        
        return True
    
    # Try backtracking with the constraints
    result_grid = backtrack(grid, 0, 0)
    
    if result_grid is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result_grid:
            print(''.join(row))
```

Wait, this backtracking might be inefficient and the constraints might not be properly handled. Let me revise with a more careful approach.

Looking at the problem again, I realize there's a simpler way. Since N is small (3-5), we can try to construct a valid solution systematically.

Key insight: For each row, we need exactly one A, one B, one C. The leftmost character must match R[i]. 

Let me implement a cleaner solution:

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Try to construct a valid grid
    # We'll use backtracking with pruning
    
    # Initialize an empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid():
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None or leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None or topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row, col):
        if row == N:
            return grid if is_valid() else None
        
        if col == N:
            return backtrack(row + 1, 0)
        
        # If current cell is already filled, move to next
        if grid[row][col] != '.':
            return backtrack(row, col + 1)
        
        # Try placing A, B, or C at this position
        # But we need to respect constraints
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
            # Check if placing char here violates constraints
            # Check row constraint: if this is the first non-empty in the row
            row_non_empty = [grid[row][j] for j in range(col) if grid[row][j] != '.']
            if len(row_non_empty) == 0 and char != R[row]:
                continue
                
            # Check column constraint: if this is the first non-empty in the column
            col_non_empty = [grid[i][col] for i in range(row) if grid[i][col] != '.']
            if len(col_non_empty) == 0 and char != C[col]:
                continue
                
            # Try placing the character
            grid[row][col] = char
            result = backtrack(row, col + 1)
            if result is not None:
                return result
        
        # Try leaving empty
        grid[row][col] = '.'
        result = backtrack(row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
            
        return None
    
    result = backtrack(0, 0)
    
    if result is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result:
            print(''.join(row))

solve()
```

    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()

    # We'll use backtracking with pruning

                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None or leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None or topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True

        if row == N:
            return grid if is_valid() else None
        
        if col == N:
            return backtrack(row + 1, 0)
        
        # If current cell is already filled, move to next
        if grid[row][col] != '.':
            return backtrack(row, col + 1)
        
        # Try placing A, B, or C at this position
        # But we need to respect constraints
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
            # Check if placing char here violates constraints
            # Check row constraint: if this is the first non-empty in the row
            row_non_empty = [grid[row][j] for j in range(col) if grid[row][j] != '.']
            if len(row_non_empty) == 0 and char != R[row]:
                continue
                
            # Check column constraint: if this is the first non-empty in the column
            col_non_empty = [grid[i][col] for i in range(row) if grid[i][col] != '.']
            if len(col_non_empty) == 0 and char != C[col]:
                continue
                
            # Try placing the character
            grid[row][col] = char
            result = backtrack(row, col + 1)
            if result is not None:
                return result
        
        # Try leaving empty
        grid[row][col] = '.'
        result = backtrack(row, col + 1)
        if result is not None:
            return result
            
        return None
    
    result = backtrack(0, 0)
    
    if result is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result:
            print(''.join(row))

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where each cell can contain A, B, C, or be empty (.)
2. Each row and each column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C
3. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i]
4. The topmost character in column i must match C[i]

## Key Insights

1. Since each row and column must have exactly one A, one B, and one C, we have a constraint that each row and column has exactly 3 non-empty cells with different letters.

2. The leftmost character constraint means that in each row i, the first non-empty cell (when scanning left to right) must be R[i].

3. The topmost character constraint means that in each column i, the first non-empty cell (when scanning top to bottom) must be C[i].

4. Given the small constraints (N ≤ 5), we can try a backtracking/brute force approach to place the letters.

## Algorithm Approach

Since N is small (3 to 5), we can use backtracking to try to fill the grid:

1. We need to place exactly one A, one B, and one C in each row and column.

2. The leftmost character constraint in each row means that if we place a character at position j in row i, and there's no character in positions 0 to j-1, then the leftmost character must be at position j and must equal R[i].

3. Similarly for columns.

4. We can try to assign positions for A, B, C in each row and column, ensuring that:
   - Each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
   - Each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
   - The leftmost character in row i is R[i]
   - The topmost character in column i is C[i]

Let me implement a solution:

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Try to find a valid arrangement using backtracking
    # We'll try to place A, B, C in each row and column
    
    # Initialize grid with all empty cells
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid():
        # Check if each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check if each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None:
                return False
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None:
                return False
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    def backtrack(pos):
        if pos == N * N:
            return is_valid()
        
        i, j = pos // N, pos % N
        
        # Try placing A, B, C, or empty
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C', '.']:
            # Check if placing this char at (i,j) causes violations
            # First check if it's reasonable given the constraints
            if char == '.':
                # If we place empty, we just need to ensure it doesn't violate 
                # the leftmost/topmost constraints
                # But empty cells are only allowed if there's already a character
                # in the row or column that's earlier (in row) or above (in column)
                # Actually, we need to ensure that the leftmost in row i remains R[i]
                # and topmost in col j remains C[j]
                
                # If this is the first cell in row i, it would become the leftmost
                # and must equal R[i]
                first_in_row = True
                for k in range(j):
                    if grid[i][k] != '.':
                        first_in_row = False
                        break
                if first_in_row and char != '.':
                    continue  # We can't place a char here if it would be first and it's not R[i]
                
                # If this is the first cell in column j, it would become topmost
                # and must equal C[j]
                first_in_col = True
                for k in range(i):
                    if grid[k][j] != '.':
                        first_in_col = False
                        break
                if first_in_col and char != '.':
                    continue  # We can't place a char here if it would be first and it's not C[j]
                
                # If we place '.', we need to ensure that there's at least one character 
                # in the row or column that satisfies the constraints
                # But actually, if we place '.', we need to make sure the leftmost/topmost
                # constraints are still satisfied by existing cells
                
                # For leftmost in row i: if j is the first position and we place '.', 
                # then we need there to be a character in positions > j
                if first_in_row and not (j < N-1 and any(grid[i][k] != '.' for k in range(j+1, N))):
                    # But actually, even if there's a character later, the leftmost will still be the first non-empty
                    # So if j is the first position and we place '.', that's fine as long as there's a non-empty cell in the row
                    # However, we need to make sure that leftmost is still R[i], so if this is the first position in row i,
                    # we need to have a non-empty cell later in the row to maintain the leftmost constraint
                    # But that might be handled by other placements
                    # For now, just place '.' and continue
                    pass
                
                # Try placing '.'
                grid[i][j] = '.'
                if backtrack(pos + 1):
                    return True
                grid[i][j] = '.'
            else:
                # Place character
                # Check if placing char at (i,j) would violate the respective constraints
                # Check if it would become the leftmost in row i
                first_in_row = True
                for k in range(j):
                    if grid[i][k] != '.':
                        first_in_row = False
                        break
                if first_in_row and char != R[i]:
                    continue
                
                # Check if it would become the topmost in column j
                first_in_col = True
                for k in range(i):
                    if grid[k][j] != '.':
                        first_in_col = False
                        break
                if first_in_col and char != C[j]:
                    continue
                
                # Check if placing this character would make row or column have duplicate characters
                row_chars = [grid[i][k] for k in range(N) if grid[i][k] != '.']
                col_chars = [grid[k][j] for k in range(N) if grid[k][j] != '.']
                
                if char in row_chars or char in col_chars:
                    continue
                
                grid[i][j] = char
                if backtrack(pos + 1):
                    return True
                grid[i][j] = '.'
        
        return False
    
    # We need to ensure each row and column has exactly 3 non-empty cells
    # But the above backtracking might not work efficiently due to the complexity
    
    # Alternative approach: Since N is small, we can try to generate all possible valid assignments
    # But let's try a more direct approach
    
    # Actually, the constraint that each row/column has exactly one A, B, C means we need exactly 3 non-empty cells per row/column
    
    # Let me try a different backtracking that ensures we place exactly 3 per row/column
    
    def is_valid_placement():
        # Check row constraints
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check column constraints
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None:
                return False
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None:
                return False
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    # Try solving with backtracking that respects the constraints
    def solve_backtrack(idx):
        if idx == N * N:
            return is_valid_placement()
        
        i, j = idx // N, idx % N
        
        # Try placing each character or empty
        # We need to ensure that placement doesn't violate the leftmost/topmost constraints
        # and doesn't create duplicate characters in rows/columns
        
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C', '.']:
            # Check if placing char at (i,j) is valid
            # If it's not '.', it must not be the first in its row or column if it doesn't match the constraint
            first_in_row = True
            for k in range(j):
                if grid[i][k] != '.':
                    first_in_row = False
                    break
            first_in_col = True
            for k in range(i):
                if grid[k][j] != '.':
                    first_in_col = False
                    break
            
            # If placing char would make it the first in row and it doesn't match constraint
            if char != '.' and first_in_row and char != R[i]:
                continue
                
            # If placing char would make it the first in column and it doesn't match constraint
            if char != '.' and first_in_col and char != C[j]:
                continue
                
            # Check row and column character counts
            row_chars = [grid[i][k] for k in range(N) if grid[i][k] != '.']
            col_chars = [grid[k][j] for k in range(N) if grid[k][j] != '.']
            
            if char in row_chars or char in col_chars:
                continue
                
            # Try placing
            grid[i][j] = char
            if solve_backtrack(idx + 1):
                return True
            grid[i][j] = '.'  # backtrack
        
        return False

    # First check basic constraints: for each row, we need to have a leftmost character
    # For each row i, R[i] must be one of A,B,C and must appear somewhere in the row
    # Similarly for columns

    # and it's the first non-empty, or there's some other arrangement
    
    # Let's pre-check: for each row, R[i] must be placed in that row, and if it's placed in position j,
    # then all positions 0 to j-1 must be empty
    
    # Actually, the leftmost character constraint is automatically satisfied if R[i] appears in the row,
    # and it's placed at the first position where it appears (or if it's the only character in that row)
    
    # But we need to ensure we can place R[i] in a valid position
    
    # Let me simplify: try to place R[i] at position 0 in each row i
    # Because if we place R[i] at position 0, then the leftmost character is automatically R[i]
    
    # Similarly, for column j, place C[j] at position 0
    
    # But we have the constraint that each row/column must have exactly one A, B, C
    
    # Let's try a direct construction:
    
    # Create a candidate solution
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # Place R[i] in row i at position 0 (if possible)
    # Place C[j] in column j at position 0 (if possible)
    
    # But position (0,0) can't be both R[0] and C[0] unless R[0]==C[0]
    
    # So we need to be careful
    
    # First check if R and C are consistent at (0,0)
    if R[0] != C[0]:
        print("No")
        return
    
    # Try placing R[i] at position 0 in row i
    # But only if it doesn't conflict with column constraints
    
    # For each row i, we need to place R[i] somewhere
    # The leftmost character constraint means that if we place R[i] at position j in row i,
    # then all positions 0 to j-1 must be empty
    
    # For each column j, we need to place C[j] somewhere
    # The topmost character constraint means that if we place C[j] at position i in column j,
    # then all positions 0 to i-1 must be empty
    
    # Let's try to solve by iteratively assigning positions
    
    # We'll try all possible permutations of positions for A, B, C in each row/column
    # Since N is small, we can try a backtracking that assigns positions to each letter
    
    # Actually, given the complexity, let me implement a simpler backtracking that tries all possibilities
    
    # We'll use a more direct backtracking with pruning
    
    def can_place(row, col, char):
        # Check if we can place char at (row, col) without violating constraints
        # For row constraints: if this is the first non-empty in row, must equal R[row]
        first_in_row = True
        for j in range(col):
            if grid[row][j] != '.':
                first_in_row = False
                break
        if char != '.' and first_in_row and char != R[row]:
            return False
            
        # For column constraints: if this is the first non-empty in column, must equal C[col]
        first_in_col = True
        for i in range(row):
            if grid[i][col] != '.':
                first_in_col = False
                break
        if char != '.' and first_in_col and char != C[col]:
            return False
            
        # Check if this would create duplicate characters in row or column
        row_chars = [grid[row][j] for j in range(N) if grid[row][j] != '.']
        col_chars = [grid[i][col] for i in range(N) if grid[i][col] != '.']
        
        if char in row_chars or char in col_chars:
            return False
            
        return True
    
    def backtrack(pos):
        if pos == N * N:
            return is_valid_placement()
        
        i, j = pos // N, pos % N
        
        # Try each character
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C', '.']:
            if can_place(i, j, char):
                grid[i][j] = char
                if backtrack(pos + 1):
                    return True
                grid[i][j] = '.'
        
        return False
    
    if backtrack(0):
        print("Yes")
        for i in range(N):
            print(''.join(grid[i]))
    else:
        print("No")
```

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Initialize grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid():

            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, B, C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost is None or leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost in each column

            topmost = None

                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost is None or topmost != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    def can_place(i, j, char):
        # Check if placing char at (i,j) is valid
        # If it's not '.', check if it would be the first non-empty in row or column
        first_in_row = True
        for k in range(j):

                first_in_row = False

        first_in_col = True

            if grid[k][j] != '.':
                first_in_col = False
                break

        if char != '.':
            if first_in_row and char != R[i]:
                return False
            if first_in_col and char != C[j]:
                return False

        col_chars = []

            if grid[k][j] != '.':
                col_chars.append(grid[k][j])

        print("No")
```

                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, B, C

                return False

                return False

        first_in_row = True
        for k in range(j):

                first_in_col = False
                break

            if first_in_row and char != R[i]:

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

\vdots

A_N

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where each cell can contain A, B, C, or be empty (.)
2. Each row and each column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C
3. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i]
4. The topmost character in column i must match C[i]

## Key Observations

1. Since each row and column must have exactly one A, one B, and one C, we must place exactly three characters per row/column (one of each letter)
2. The leftmost character constraint means that in each row, the first non-empty cell must match the corresponding character in R
3. Similarly, in each column, the first non-empty cell must match the corresponding character in C

## Strategy

This is a constraint satisfaction problem. Since N is small (3 to 5), we can try a backtracking approach or use logical deduction.

However, let me think about constraints that make it impossible:

1. The leftmost character in row i must be R[i]. This means R[i] must appear in that row, and it must be the first non-empty cell.
2. Similarly, C[i] must appear in column i, and it must be the first non-empty cell in that column.

For a solution to exist:
- If R[i] is 'A', then the first non-empty cell in row i must be 'A'
- If R[i] is 'B', then the first non-empty cell in row i must be 'B'
- etc.

Also, since each row has exactly three non-empty cells (one A, one B, one C), and the first non-empty cell has a specific value, we need to ensure that the remaining two cells can provide the missing letters.

## Algorithm Approach

Given the small constraints (N ≤ 5), I'll use a backtracking approach with pruning:

1. Initialize an empty grid
2. For each row, determine where the leftmost character should go based on R[i]
3. For each column, determine where the topmost character should go based on C[i]
4. Use backtracking to assign positions for A, B, C such that:
   - Each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
   - Each column has exactly one A, one B, one C  
   - The leftmost non-empty cell in each row matches R[i]
   - The topmost non-empty cell in each column matches C[i]

For each row i:
- The leftmost non-empty cell must be R[i]
- This means if R[i] = 'A', then the first non-empty cell in row i must be 'A'
- Similarly for B and C

For each column j:
- The topmost non-empty cell must be C[j]
- This means if C[j] = 'A', then the first non-empty cell in column j must be 'A'
- etc.

This creates constraints on the positions.

Since N is very small (3-5), I can try to construct a solution directly by considering all possibilities, but that might be complex.

Alternative approach: use backtracking with constraint propagation.

Let me implement a backtracking solution:

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Grid to store the result
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # Helper function to check if current assignment is valid
    def is_valid():
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            row_chars_count = {}
            for c in row_chars:
                row_chars_count[c] = row_chars_count.get(c, 0) + 1
            if row_chars_count.get('A', 0) != 1 or row_chars_count.get('B', 0) != 1 or row_chars_count.get('C', 0) != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            col_chars_count = {}
            for c in col_chars:
                col_chars_count[c] = col_chars_count.get(c, 0) + 1
            if col_chars_count.get('A', 0) != 1 or col_chars_count.get('B', 0) != 1 or col_chars_count.get('C', 0) != 1:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost characters in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost characters in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    # Backtracking function
    def backtrack(idx):
        if idx == N * N:
            return is_valid()
        
        # Current position
        i, j = idx // N, idx % N
        
        # Try placing A, B, C, or empty
        for c in ['.', 'A', 'B', 'C']:
            # Check if placing c at (i,j) is valid
            # First, check if it would violate row constraints
            # For row i, if we're placing a character that's not the leftmost, 
            # it's okay as long as there's no previous non-empty character
            # But we need to be careful about the leftmost constraint
            
            # Actually, we need to think about the leftmost constraint:
            # If we place a character at (i,j), and there's already a non-empty character 
            # at (i,k) where k < j, then this would make (i,k) the leftmost, 
            # which might violate R[i] if that character doesn't match.
            
            # But in our backtracking, we're building from top-left to bottom-right,
            # so if we place a character at (i,j), any non-empty cell at (i,k) where k < j 
            # would have been placed already, so we need to ensure that the first character 
            # in the row matches R[i].
            
            # We need to check if placing c at (i,j) violates any constraint
            
            # Check if this placement violates the leftmost constraint for row i
            row_leftmost = None
            for k in range(j):
                if grid[i][k] != '.':
                    row_leftmost = grid[i][k]
                    break
            if row_leftmost is not None and row_leftmost != R[i]:
                continue
                
            # If we're placing the first non-empty character in row i, it must match R[i]
            if row_leftmost is None and c != '.':
                if c != R[i]:
                    continue
            
            # Check if this placement violates the topmost constraint for column j
            col_topmost = None
            for k in range(i):
                if grid[k][j] != '.':
                    col_topmost = grid[k][j]
                    break
            if col_topmost is not None and col_topmost != C[j]:
                continue
                
            # If we're placing the first non-empty character in column j, it must match C[j]
            if col_topmost is None and c != '.':
                if c != C[j]:
                    continue
            
            # Try placing c at (i,j)
            grid[i][j] = c
            
            # Continue backtracking
            if backtrack(idx + 1):
                return True
                
            # Backtrack
            grid[i][j] = '.'
            
        return False
    
    # Try to find a solution
    if backtrack(0):
        print("Yes")
        for row in grid:
            print(''.join(row))
    else:
        print("No")

solve()
```

But in each row, we need exactly one A, one B, one C. So the leftmost character must be one of A, B, C.

Similarly for columns.

But there's another constraint: if in row i, R[i] = 'A', then the first non-empty cell must be 'A'. This means that A appears at the first position of that row.

Similarly, if R[i] = 'B', then B appears at the first position of that row.

This gives us exact positions for certain characters.

However, the backtracking approach should work given the small constraints. Let me fix the code:

```python
N = int(input())
R = input().strip()
C = input().strip()

# Grid to store the result
grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]

def is_valid():
    # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for i in range(N):
        row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
        if len(row_chars) != 3:
            return False
        row_chars_count = {}
        for c in row_chars:
            row_chars_count[c] = row_chars_count.get(c, 0) + 1
        if row_chars_count.get('A', 0) != 1 or row_chars_count.get('B', 0) != 1 or row_chars_count.get('C', 0) != 1:
            return False
            
    # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for j in range(N):
        col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
        if len(col_chars) != 3:
            return False
        col_chars_count = {}
        for c in col_chars:
            col_chars_count[c] = col_chars_count.get(c, 0) + 1
        if col_chars_count.get('A', 0) != 1 or col_chars_count.get('B', 0) != 1 or col_chars_count.get('C', 0) != 1:
            return False
        
    # Check leftmost characters in each row
    for i in range(N):
        leftmost = None
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                leftmost = grid[i][j]
                break
        if leftmost != R[i]:
            return False
        
    # Check topmost characters in each column
    for j in range(N):
        topmost = None
        for i in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                topmost = grid[i][j]
                break
        if topmost != C[j]:
            return False
            
    return True

def backtrack(idx):
    if idx == N * N:
        return is_valid()
    
    i, j = idx // N, idx % N
    
    # Try each possible character at (i, j)
    for c in ['.']:
        # We need to be careful about the leftmost and topmost constraints
        # First, check if placing '.' is valid
        grid[i][j] = c
        
        if backtrack(idx + 1):
            return True
            
        grid[i][j] = '.'
    
    # Try A, B, C - but only if they don't violate constraints
    for c in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
        # Check row constraint - if this is the first non-empty cell in row i
        row_has_non_empty = any(grid[i][k] != '.' for k in range(j))
        if row_has_non_empty:
            # There's already a non-empty cell in this row at col < j
            # So the leftmost cell is already determined
            leftmost_char = None
            for k in range(j):
                if grid[i][k] != '.':
                    leftmost_char = grid[i][k]
                    break
            if leftmost_char != R[i]:
                continue
        else:
            # This is the first non-empty cell in row i
            if c != R[i]:
                continue
        
        # Check column constraint - if this is the first non-empty cell in column j
        col_has_non_empty = any(grid[k][j] != '.' for k in range(i))
        if col_has_non_empty:
            # There's already a non-empty cell in this column at row < i
            # So the topmost cell is already determined
            topmost_char = None
            for k in range(i):
                if grid[k][j] != '.':
                    topmost_char = grid[k][j]
                    break
            if topmost_char != C[j]:
                continue
        else:
            # This is the first non-empty cell in column j
            if c != C[j]:
                continue
        
        # Try placing c
        grid[i][j] = c
        
        if backtrack(idx + 1):
            return True
            
        grid[i][j] = '.'
    
    return False

# Start backtracking
if backtrack(0):
    print("Yes")
    for row in grid:
        print(''.join(row))
else:
    print("No")
```

```python
        for c in row_chars:
            count[c] += 1
        if count['A'] != 1 or count['B'] != 1 or count['C'] != 1:
            return False
            
    # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for j in range(N):
        col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
        if len(col_chars) != 3:
            return False

            count[c] += 1
        if count['A'] != 1 or count['B'] != 1 or count['C'] != 1:
            return False
            
    # Check leftmost characters in each row
    for i in range(N):
        leftmost = None
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                leftmost = grid[i][j]
                break
        if leftmost != R[i]:
            return False
            
    # Check topmost characters in each column

        topmost = None

            return False

def backtrack(pos):
    if pos == N * N:
        return is_valid()
    
    i, j = pos // N, pos % N
    
    # Try each possible character at (i, j)
    for c in ['.', 'A', 'B', 'C']:
        # Check constraints before placing
        # Check row: if there's already a non-empty char in this row at col < j, 
        # then the leftmost char must match R[i]

            for k in range(j):
                if grid[i][k] != '.':
                    leftmost_val = grid[i][k]
                    break
            if leftmost_val != R[i]:
                continue
        else:
            # This is the first non-empty cell in row i
            if c != R[i] and c != '.':
                continue
                
        # Check column: if there's already a non-empty char in this col at row < i, 
        # then the topmost char must match C[j]
        col_has_top = any(grid[k][j] != '.' for k in range(i))
        if col_has_top:
            # The topmost char in col j is already determined
            topmost_val = None
            for k in range(i):
                if grid[k][j] != '.':
                    topmost_val = grid[k][j]
                    break
            if topmost_val != C[j]:
                continue
        else:
            # This is the first non-empty cell in col j
            if c != C[j] and c != '.':
                continue
                
        # Place the character
        grid[i][j] = c
        
        # Recurse
        if backtrack(pos + 1):
            return True
            
        # Backtrack
        grid[i][j] = '.'
    
    return False

if backtrack(0):
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_1

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

Sample Input 1

5

ABCBC

ACAAB

Sample Output 1

Yes

AC..B

.BA.C

C.BA.

BA.C.

..CBA


The grid in the output example satisfies all the following conditions, so it will be treated as correct.

- Each row contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- Each column contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost characters written in the rows are A, B, C, B, C from top to bottom.
- The topmost characters written in the columns are A, C, A, A, B from left to right.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where each cell can contain A, B, C, or be empty (.)
2. Each row and each column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C
3. The leftmost non-empty character in row i must match R[i]
4. The topmost non-empty character in column i must match C[i]
5. We need to determine if such a configuration is possible and output one valid solution

## Key Observations

1. Since each row and column must have exactly one A, one B, and one C, there are exactly 3 non-empty cells per row and per column
2. The leftmost character in each row gives us a constraint on where A, B, or C appears in that row
3. The topmost character in each column gives us a constraint on where A, B, or C appears in that column
4. We need to place exactly one A, one B, and one C in each row and column

## Solution Strategy

Since N is small (3 to 5), we can use backtracking to try all possible placements. However, we can also reason through the constraints:

1. For each row i, the leftmost non-empty character must be R[i]
2. For each column j, the topmost non-empty character must be C[j]
3. We need to ensure every row and column has exactly one A, one B, and one C

## Step-by-step Approach

1. First, check if the constraints are possible:
   - For each row i, if R[i] is A, then A must be the leftmost character in that row
   - For each column j, if C[j] is A, then A must be the topmost character in that column
   - This means we can't have a situation where R[i] = A but there's no A in column j where C[j] = A, etc.

2. We need to place the three characters (A, B, C) such that:
   - In each row, the leftmost character is R[i]
   - In each column, the topmost character is C[j]
   - Each row and column has exactly one of each character

3. We can use a constructive approach:
   - Place the required leftmost characters first
   - Then place the remaining characters to satisfy the column constraints
   - Check that we end up with exactly one A, B, C per row and column

Let me implement this with backtracking since N is small:

```python
from itertools import permutations

def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Try all possible positions for the three characters in each row
    # We need to ensure each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
    # And each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    # And the leftmost in each row matches R[i], topmost in each column matches C[j]
    
    # Generate all possible placements of A/B/C in each row
    # Each row needs exactly one A, one B, one C
    
    def is_valid_placement(grid):
        # Check if each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check if each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    # Generate all possible grid configurations that satisfy row constraints
    # For each row, we need to place A, B, C in 3 different columns
    from itertools import combinations
    
    def generate_row_placements():
        placements = []
        for row_index in range(N):
            # Find all possible positions for A, B, C in this row
            # The leftmost character must be R[row_index]
            leftmost_char = R[row_index]
            
            # We need to place A, B, C in 3 different columns
            # The leftmost non-empty cell must have the character leftmost_char
            for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
                # Check if leftmost position has the right character
                # The leftmost position in this row will be min(cols)
                leftmost_pos = min(cols)
                if grid[row_index][leftmost_pos] != leftmost_char:
                    continue
                    
                # Create a row with A, B, C at the specified positions
                row = ['.'] * N
                for col in cols:
                    row[col] = leftmost_char if col == leftmost_pos else None
                # We need to assign A, B, C to the three positions
                # But we know the leftmost has the required character
                # The other two positions should have the remaining two characters
                chars = ['A', 'B', 'C']
                chars.remove(leftmost_char)
                remaining_positions = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
                if len(remaining_positions) == 2:
                    # Assign the remaining two characters to the two positions
                    for pos, char in zip(remaining_positions, chars):
                        row[pos] = char
                placements.append(row)
        return placements
    
    # Better approach: use backtracking to build the grid
    def backtrack(row_idx, grid):
        if row_idx == N:
            # Check if the current grid satisfies all constraints
            return is_valid_placement(grid)
            
        # Try placing A, B, C in different positions in this row
        # The leftmost non-empty cell must be R[row_idx]
        row = grid[row_idx]
        leftmost_char = R[row_idx]
        
        # Try all possible combinations of 3 columns for this row
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if the leftmost position (min(cols)) has the required character
            leftmost_pos = min(cols)
            if leftmost_pos < N:
                # Create a new row with A, B, C at the selected positions
                new_row = ['.'] * N
                chars = ['A', 'B', 'C']
                # The leftmost position must have the required character
                if leftmost_char not in chars:
                    continue
                chars.remove(leftmost_char)
                # Assign the remaining two characters to the other two positions
                other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
                if len(other_pos) == 2:
                    # Assign the characters
                    for pos, char in zip(other_pos, chars):
                        new_row[pos] = char
                    new_row[leftmost_pos] = leftmost_char
                    
                    # Check if this placement violates column constraints
                    # Create a temporary grid and check column constraints
                    temp_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                    temp_grid[row_idx] = new_row
                    
                    # Check if this placement violates column constraints
                    # We'll check in the next step
                    if backtrack(row_idx + 1, temp_grid):
                        return True
                        
        return False
    
    # The above backtracking is getting complex. Let's try a more direct approach.
    
    # Since N is very small (3-5), we can try all possible ways to place the three characters
    # in each row such that the leftmost constraint is satisfied, then check column constraints.
    
    def try_all_compositions():
        # Try all possible ways to assign positions to A, B, C in each row
        # For each row, try all combinations of 3 columns, and ensure leftmost constraint
        
        from itertools import product, combinations
        
        # Generate all possible valid configurations
        valid_configs = []
        
        # For each row, generate all valid position combinations
        for row_idx in range(N):
            valid_positions_for_row = []
            rows = []
            for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
                leftmost_pos = min(cols)
                leftmost_char = R[row_idx]
                if leftmost_char == 'A':
                    if leftmost_pos < N:
                        # Create a row configuration
                        row = ['.'] * N
                        chars = ['A', 'B', 'C']
                        chars.remove('A')
                        # Place A at leftmost position
                        row[leftmost_pos] = 'A'
                        # Place B and C at other two positions
                        other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
                        if len(other_pos) == 2:
                            row[other_pos[0]] = chars[0]
                            row[other_pos[1]] = chars[1]
                            rows.append(row[:])
                elif leftmost_char == 'B':
                    if leftmost_pos < N:
                        row = ['.'] * N
                        chars = ['A', 'B', 'C']
                        chars.remove('B')
                        row[leftmost_pos] = 'B'
                        other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
                        if len(other_pos) == 2:
                            row[other_pos[0]] = chars[0]
                            row[other_pos[1]] = chars[1]
                            rows.append(row[:])
                elif leftmost_char == 'C':
                    if leftmost_pos < N:
                        row = ['.'] * N
                        chars = ['A', 'B', 'C']
                        chars.remove('C')
                        row[leftmost_pos] = 'C'
                        other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
                        if len(other_pos) == 2:
                            row[other_pos[0]] = chars[0]
                            row[other_pos[1]] = chars[1]
                            rows.append(row[:])
                else:
                    continue
            
            if not rows:
                return None
                
            # For this row, we have multiple possibilities. We'll need to combine with others.
            valid_configs.append(rows)
        
        # Now try all combinations of valid position assignments for each row
        # using backtracking
        def backtrack_rows(row_idx, current_grid):
            if row_idx == N:
                # Check if the current grid satisfies all constraints
                return is_valid_placement(current_grid)
                
            # Try each valid configuration for this row
            for row_config in valid_configs[row_idx]:
                new_grid = [row[:] for row in current_grid]
                new_grid[row_idx] = row_config
                if backtrack_rows(row_idx + 1, new_grid):
                    return True
                    
            return False
        
        # Try to find a valid configuration
        initial_grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
        if backtrack_rows(0, initial_grid):
            return initial_grid
        else:
            return None
    
    # Given the complexity, let's implement a simpler but correct backtracking
    result = try_all_compositions()
    
    if result is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result:
            print(''.join(row))

solve()
```

```python
def solve():

    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # We'll use backtracking to try all possible placements
    # Each row must have exactly one A, one B, one C
    # The leftmost character in row i must be R[i]
    # The topmost character in column j must be C[j]
    
    def is_valid(grid):
        # Check rows

            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:

            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check columns
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check topmost in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row, grid):
        if row == N:
            return is_valid(grid)
            
        # Try all possible positions for A, B, C in this row
        # The leftmost character must be R[row]
        leftmost_char = R[row]
        
        # Try all combinations of 3 columns for this row
        from itertools import combinations
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if leftmost position has required character
            leftmost_pos = min(cols)
            
            # Create a new row configuration
            row_config = ['.'] * N
            remaining_chars = []
            if leftmost_char == 'A':
                remaining_chars = ['B', 'C']
            elif leftmost_char == 'B':
                remaining_chars = ['A', 'C']
            elif leftmost_char == 'C':
                remaining_chars = ['A', 'B']
            else:
                continue
                
            # Assign to positions
            row_config[leftmost_pos] = leftmost_char
            other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
            if len(other_pos) == 2:
                # Assign the remaining characters
                for pos, char in zip(other_pos, remaining_chars):
                    row_config[pos] = char
                    
                # Try this configuration
                new_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                new_grid[row] = row_config
                
                if backtrack(row + 1, new_grid):
                    return True
                    
        return False
    
    # Initialize empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    if backtrack(0, grid):
        print("Yes")
        for row in grid:
            print(''.join(row))
    else:
        print("No")
```

def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # We'll use backtracking to try all possible placements
    # Each row must have exactly one A, one B, one C
    # The leftmost character in row i must be R[i]
    # The topmost character in column j must be C[j]
    
    def is_valid(grid):
        # Check rows
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check columns
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = None
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if leftmost != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check topmost in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = None
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = grid[i][j]
                    break
            if topmost != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row, grid):
        if row == N:
            return is_valid(grid)
            
        # Try all possible positions for A, B, C in this row
        # The leftmost character must be R[row]
        leftmost_char = R[row]
        
        # Try all combinations of 3 columns for this row
        from itertools import combinations
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if leftmost position has required character
            leftmost_pos = min(cols)
            
            # Create a new row configuration
            row_config = ['.'] * N
            remaining_chars = []
            if leftmost_char == 'A':
                remaining_chars = ['B', 'C']
            elif leftmost_char == 'B':
                remaining_chars = ['A', 'C']
            elif leftmost_char == 'C':
                remaining_chars = ['A', 'B']
            else:
                continue
                
            # Assign to positions
            row_config[leftmost_pos] = leftmost_char
            other_pos = [c for c in cols if c != leftmost_pos]
            if len(other_pos) == 2:
                # Assign the remaining characters
                for pos, char in zip(other_pos, remaining_chars):
                    row_config[pos] = char
                    
                # Try this configuration
                new_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                new_grid[row] = row_config
                
                if backtrack(row + 1, new_grid):
                    return True
                    
        return False
    
    # Initialize empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    if backtrack(0, grid):
        print("Yes")
        for row in grid:
            print(''.join(row))
    else:
        print("No")

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_1

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

Sample Input 1

ACAAB

Sample Output 1

Yes

..CBA

- Each row contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- Each column contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost characters written in the rows are A, B, C, B, C from top to bottom.
- The topmost characters written in the columns are A, C, A, A, B from left to right.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where we need to place exactly one A, one B, and one C in each row and each column
2. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i]
3. The topmost character in column i must match C[i]
4. We need to determine if such a configuration exists and output one valid arrangement

## Key Insights

1. Since each row and column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C, we have constraints on where these characters can be placed.

2. The leftmost character constraint means that in row i, the first non-empty cell must contain R[i].

3. The topmost character constraint means that in column i, the first non-empty cell must contain C[i].

4. For a solution to exist:
   - The first positions (leftmost in rows, topmost in columns) must be compatible
   - The character constraints must be consistent across the grid

## Algorithm Approach

Since N is small (3 to 5), we can use backtracking with constraint propagation.

However, let's think about the constraints more systematically:

1. Each row must have exactly one A, one B, one C
2. Each column must have exactly one A, one B, one C
3. The leftmost character in row i is R[i]
4. The topmost character in column i is C[i]

The key insight is that we can place the required character in the "first position" of each row/column, and then place the remaining characters in the remaining positions.

Let me implement a solution that:
1. First checks if the basic constraints are possible
2. Uses backtracking to try different arrangements
3. For each row, place the required leftmost character in the first available column
4. For each column, ensure the topmost character constraint is satisfied
5. Then place the remaining A, B, C in the remaining cells

But actually, let's think simpler: we need to place exactly one A, B, C per row and column. This is similar to a Latin square with additional constraints.

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Initialize grid with empty cells
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # Track remaining counts of A, B, C in each row and column
    row_remaining = [3] * N  # Each row needs 1 A, 1 B, 1 C
    col_remaining = [3] * N

    # For each column, we need to place the topmost character to match C[i]

        # Check row constraints
        if row_remaining[row] == 0:
            return False
        # Check column constraints  
        if col_remaining[col] == 0:
            return False

        # For column constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in the column, it must be C[col]

        first_in_row = True

            if grid[row][j] != '.':
                first_in_row = False
                break
                
        if first_in_row and char != R[row]:
            return False
            
        # Check if any cell above (row, col) in the same column already has a character
        first_in_col = True

            if grid[i][col] != '.':
                first_in_col = False
                break
                
        if first_in_col and char != C[col]:
            return False
            
        return True

        # But we need to make sure these don't conflict

        # For each row i, we need to place R[i] at some position
        # For each column i, we need to place C[i] at some position
        
        # This seems complex. Let me implement a complete solution with backtracking
        # that considers all possible placements for A, B, C
```
