```python
    # Actually, given the small constraints, I'll use a simpler backtracking
    # Try to place each cell with A, B, C, or empty, ensuring constraints
    
    def can_place():
        # First, let's check if the leftmost and topmost constraints can be satisfied
        # We need to ensure that for each row i, R[i] appears in the row as its leftmost character
        # For each column i, C[i] appears as its topmost character
        
        # Try to construct a solution by placing the required characters in their "first" positions
        # and then filling the rest
        
        # Actually, let me try a direct construction approach for small N
        
        # We'll try to assign positions for A, B, C such that:
        # - In row i, there's exactly one A, one B, one C
        # - In col j, there's exactly one A, one B, one C  
        # - The leftmost non-empty cell in row i is R[i]
        # - The topmost non-empty cell in col j is C[j]
        
        # Since N is small, we can try to place the first character in each row and column
        
        grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
        
        # For each row, we need to place R[i] in the leftmost position of that row
        # For each column, we need to place C[i] in the topmost position of that column
        
        # Try to assign positions where R[i] appears in row i
        # And C[j] appears in column j
        
        # But these positions might conflict
        
        # Let's try all possible assignments for positions of the "first" characters
        # Since N is at most 5, we can try a backtracking search
        
        def backtrack_pos(pos):
            if pos == N:
                # Try to finish the grid with the remaining characters
                # First check if we have enough A, B, C in each row and column
                row_counts = [[0,0,0] for _ in range(N)]
                col_counts = [[0,0,0] for _ in range(N)]
                
                for i in range(N):
                    for j in range(N):
                        if grid[i][j] != '.':
                            row_counts[i][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
                            col_counts[j][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
                
                # Check if each row has exactly one A, B, C
                for i in range(N):
                    if row_counts[i][0] != 1 or row_counts[i][1] != 1 or row_counts[i][2] != 1:
                        return False
                
                # Check if each column has exactly one A, B, C
                for j in range(N):
                    if col_counts[j][0] != 1 or col_counts[j][1] != 1 or col_counts[j][2] != 1:
                        return False

                        return False

                        return False

                    topmost = -1
                    for i in range(N):
                        if grid[i][j] != '.':
                            if topmost == -1:
                                topmost = i
                            break
                    if topmost == -1:
                        return False
                    if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                        return False
                
                return True
            
            # Try to place a character at position (pos, pos) - this is row pos, col pos
            # But we need to try all N positions in row pos
            for col in range(N):
                # Try placing A, B, C at (pos, col) or leave empty
                # But we need to ensure the leftmost/topmost constraints
                
                # First, check if placing a character at (pos, col) would violate constraints
                # For row pos: if it's the first non-empty cell in row pos, it should be R[pos]
                # For col col: if it's the first non-empty cell in col col, it should be C[col]
                
                # Check if any cell to the left of col in row pos already has a character
                first_in_row = True
                for j in range(col):
                    if grid[pos][j] != '.':
                        first_in_row = False
                        break
                
                # Check if any cell above col in col col already has a character
                first_in_col = True
                for i in range(pos):
                    if grid[i][col] != '.':
                        first_in_col = False
                        break
                
                # Try placing A, B, C at (pos, col)
                for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
                    if first_in_row and char != R[pos]:
                        continue
                    if first_in_col and char != C[col]:
                        continue
                    
                    # Place the character
                    grid[pos][col] = char
                    
                    # Recurse
                    if backtrack_pos(pos + 1):
                        return True
                    
                    # Backtrack
                    grid[pos][col] = '.'
                
                # Try leaving the cell empty
                # If we're not placing a character, then we continue to next position
                # But we need to ensure that we still have valid leftmost/topmost constraints
                # We can skip this and handle it in the recursion
                
            return False
        
        # Start backtracking from (0,0)
        result = backtrack_pos(0)
        return result
    
    # Actually, I think this is getting too complex for the constraints
    # Let me try a simpler direct construction
    
    # Since N is small (3-5), we can try to place the required characters first
    # and then fill the rest
    
    # For each row i, find a column j where we can place R[i] such that:
    # - It's the leftmost in the row (no non-empty cell to the left)
    # - The column j has C[j] as its first non-empty character
    
    # But this might be too restrictive
    
    # Let me implement a complete backtracking solution that tries all assignments
    
    def solve_backtrack():
        # Use backtracking to place characters
        grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
        
        def is_valid(row, col, char):
            # Check row constraints
            # If this is the first non-empty cell in row, it must be R[row]
            first_in_row = True
            for j in range(col):
                if grid[row][j] != '.':
                    first_in_row = False
                    break
            if first_in_row and char != R[row]:
                return False
                
            # Check column constraints
            first_in_col = True
            for i in range(row):
                if grid[i][col] != '.':
                    first_in_col = False
                    break
            if first_in_col and char != C[col]:
                return False
                
            return True
        
        def backtrack(r, c):
            if r == N:
                # Check if all rows and columns have exactly one A, B, C
                row_counts = [0, 0, 0] * N
                col_counts = [0, 0, 0] * N
                for i in range(N):
                    for j in range(N):
                        if grid[i][j] != '.':
                            row_counts[i][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
                            col_counts[j][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
                
                for i in range(N):
                    if row_counts[i][0] != 1 or row_counts[i][1] != 1 or row_counts[i][2] != 1:
                        return False
                for j in range(N):
                    if col_counts[j][0] != 1 or col_counts[j][1] != 1 or col_counts[j][2] != 1:
                        return False
                
                # Check leftmost and topmost constraints
                for i in range(N):
                    leftmost = -1
                    for j in range(N):
                        if grid[i][j] != '.':
                            if leftmost == -1:
                                leftmost = j
                            break
                    if leftmost == -1:
                        return False
                    if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                        return False
                
                for j in range(N):

                    for i in range(N):

                                topmost = i
                            break
                    if topmost == -1:
                        return False
                    if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                        return False
                
                return True

                return backtrack(r + 1, 0)

                return backtrack(r, c + 1)

                if is_valid(r, c, char):

                    if backtrack(r, c + 1):
                        return True

            # But if we leave empty, we don't need to check validity
            # We'll continue to the next cell
            return False

        if backtrack(0, 0):
            return True, grid
        else:
            return False, None

    success, grid = solve_backtrack()
    
    if success:
        print("Yes")
        for row in grid:
            print(''.join(row))
    else:
        print("No")
```

Wait, I see an issue with my backtracking - I'm not properly handling the cases where we leave cells empty. Also, the complexity might be too high, and there might be a more direct approach.

Let me reconsider the problem. Since each row and column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C, we can think of this as placing the positions for A, B, C in each row and column.

The key constraints are:
- In row i, the leftmost non-empty cell must be R[i]
- In column j, the topmost non-empty cell must be C[j]

This means that for row i, if we place R[i] at column k, then all columns < k must be empty in row i.

Similarly, for column j, if we place C[j] at row k, then all rows < k must be empty in column j.

So we need to assign positions to each of the A, B, C in each row and column, with these constraints.

But since there are exactly 3 characters per row, we can try to assign the positions systematically.

However, given the complexity and small N, let me implement the backtracking solution more carefully:

```python
N = int(input())
R = input().strip()
C = input().strip()

# Backtracking to find a valid assignment
grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]

def is_valid_placement(row, col, char):
    # Check if placing char at (row, col) violates row or column first constraint
    # Check row first constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in row, it must be R[row]
    first_in_row = True
    for j in range(col):
        if grid[row][j] != '.':
            first_in_row = False
            break
    if first_in_row and char != R[row]:
        return False
        
    # Check column first constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in col, it must be C[col]
    first_in_col = True
    for i in range(row):
        if grid[i][col] != '.':
            first_in_col = False
            break
    if first_in_col and char != C[col]:
        return False
        
    return True

def backtrack(r, c):
    # Base case: we've filled all cells
    if r == N:
        # Verify all rows and columns have exactly one A, one B, one C
        row_chars = []
        col_chars = []
        
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars.append([grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.'])
            col_chars.append([grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.'])
        
        # Check if each row has exactly one A, B, C
        for i in range(N):
            row_content = row_chars[i]
            if len(row_content) != 3:
                return False
            if row_content.count('A') != 1 or row_content.count('B') != 1 or row_content.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check if each column has exactly one A, B, C
        for j in range(N):
            col_content = col_chars[j]
            if len(col_content) != 3:
                return False
            if col_content.count('A') != 1 or col_content.count('B') != 1 or col_content.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
        # Check leftmost character in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    if leftmost == -1:
                        leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check topmost character in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    if topmost == -1:
                        topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    # If we've filled a complete row, move to next row
    if c == N:
        return backtrack(r + 1, 0)
    
    # If current cell is already filled, move to next cell
    if grid[r][c] != '.':
        return backtrack(r, c + 1)
    
    # Try placing A, B, or C at (r, c)
    for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
        if is_valid_placement(r, c, char):
            grid[r][c] = char
            if backtrack(r, c + 1):
                return True
            grid[r][c] = '.'
    
    # Try leaving empty
    # We don't need to explicitly try leaving empty because if we don't place anything,
    # the cell remains empty and we'll try the next cell
    return False

# Try to solve
if backtrack(0, 0):
    print("Yes")
    for row in grid:
        print(''.join(row))
else:
    print("No")
```

# Backtracking to find a valid assignment
grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]

def is_valid_placement(row, col, char):
    # Check if placing char at (row, col) violates row or column first constraint
    # Check row first constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in row, it must be R[row]
    first_in_row = True
    for j in range(col):
        if grid[row][j] != '.':
            first_in_row = False
            break
    if first_in_row and char != R[row]:
        return False
        
    # Check column first constraint: if this is the first non-empty cell in col, it must be C[col]
    first_in_col = True
    for i in range(row):
        if grid[i][col] != '.':
            first_in_col = False
            break
    if first_in_col and char != C[col]:
        return False
        
    return True

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_1

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

Sample Input 1

5

ABCBC

ACAAB

Sample Output 1

Yes

AC..B

.BA.C

C.BA.

BA.C.

..CBA


The grid in the output example satisfies all the following conditions, so it will be treated as correct.

- Each row contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- Each column contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost characters written in the rows are A, B, C, B, C from top to bottom.
- The topmost characters written in the columns are A, C, A, A, B from left to right.

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Initialize grid with empty cells
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # Try to place first characters
    # For row i, place R[i] at the first possible position
    # For col j, place C[j] at the first possible position
    
    # We need to find positions for R[i] and C[j] such that:
    # If R[i] is placed at (i, pos), then C[pos] must be R[i]
    # If C[j] is placed at (pos, j), then R[pos] must be C[j]
    
    # This means that for any position (i,j), if we place R[i] at j, then C[j] must be R[i]
    # And if we place C[j] at i, then R[i] must be C[j]
    
    # The compatible assignments must satisfy these constraints
    
    # Try all possible ways to assign positions for the first characters
    
    def is_valid_placement(pos_row, pos_col):
        # Check if the leftmost and topmost constraints are satisfied
        # For row pos_row, the leftmost character should be R[pos_row]
        # For col pos_col, the topmost character should be C[pos_col]
        return True
    
    def check_column_counts(grid):
        count = [[0, 0, 0] for _ in range(N)]  # count[ri][c] = count of A,B,C in row ri
        for i in range(N):
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    count[i][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
        for i in range(N):
            if count[i][0] != 1 or count[i][1] != 1 or count[i][2] != 1:
                return False
        return True
    
    def check_row_counts(grid):
        count = [[0, 0, 0] for _ in range(N)]
        for j in range(N):
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    count[j][ord(grid[i][j]) - ord('A')] += 1
        for j in range(N):
            if count[j][0] != 1 or count[j][1] != 1 or count[j][2] != 1:
                return False
        return True
    
    def check_leftmost(grid):
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    if leftmost == -1:
                        leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost != -1:
                if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                    return False
        return True
    
    def check_topmost(grid):
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    if topmost == -1:
                        topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost != -1:
                if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                    return False
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row, col):
        if row == N:
            # Check if all constraints are satisfied
            if check_column_counts(grid) and check_row_counts(grid) and check_leftmost(grid) and check_topmost(grid):
                return True
            return False
        
        if col == N:
            return backtrack(row + 1, 0)
        
        # Try empty cell
        if backtrack(row, col + 1):
            return True
        
        # Try A, B, C
        for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
            # Check if placing char at (row, col) violates constraints
            # First, check if it would make leftmost or topmost invalid
            # Check row constraints - before placing, the row's leftmost might be changed
            # But we need to consider the current state
            # We need to ensure that if this is the first non-empty cell in row, it matches R[row]
            # Check if this cell is the first non-empty in row
            is_first_in_row = True
            for j in range(col):
                if grid[row][j] != '.':
                    is_first_in_row = False
                    break
            
            # Check if this cell is the first non-empty in column
            is_first_in_col = True
            for i in range(row):
                if grid[i][col] != '.':
                    is_first_in_col = False
                    break
            
            # If it's the first in row and char != R[row], invalid
            if is_first_in_row and char != R[row]:
                continue
                
            # If it's the first in col and char != C[col], invalid
            if is_first_in_col and char != C[col]:
                continue
                
            # Place the character
            grid[row][col] = char
            
            # Continue backtracking
            if backtrack(row, col + 1):
                return True
                
            # Backtrack
            grid[row][col] = '.'
            
        return False
    
    # Try to find a valid configuration
    if backtrack(0, 0):
        print("Yes")
        for i in range(N):
            print(''.join(grid[i]))
    else:
        print("No")
```

```python
N = int(input())
R = input().strip()
C = input().strip()

# Initialize grid with empty cells
grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]

def check_constraints():
    # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for i in range(N):
        row_chars = []
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                row_chars.append(grid[i][j])
        if len(row_chars) != 3:
            return False
        if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
            return False
    
    # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for j in range(N):
        col_chars = []
        for i in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                col_chars.append(grid[i][j])
        if len(col_chars) != 3:
            return False
        if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
            return False
    
    # Check leftmost constraints
    for i in range(N):
        leftmost_col = -1
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                if leftmost_col == -1:
                    leftmost_col = j
                break
        if leftmost_col != -1 and grid[i][leftmost_col] != R[i]:
            return False
    
    # Check topmost constraints
    for j in range(N):
        topmost_row = -1
        for i in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                if topmost_row == -1:
                    topmost_row = i
                break
        if topmost_row != -1 and grid[topmost_row][j] != C[j]:
            return False
    
    return True

def backtrack(row, col):
    if row == N:
        return check_constraints()
    
    if col == N:
        return backtrack(row + 1, 0)
    
    # Try empty cell
    if backtrack(row, col + 1):
        return True
    
    # Try A, B, C
    for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
        # Check if placing char at (row, col) would violate the constraints
        # Check if this placement makes the leftmost cell invalid
        is_first_in_row = True
        for j in range(col):
            if grid[row][j] != '.':
                is_first_in_row = False
                break
        
        # Check if this placement makes the topmost cell invalid
        is_first_in_col = True
        for i in range(row):
            if grid[i][col] != '.':
                is_first_in_col = False
                break
        
        # If it's the first in row and char is not R[row], invalid
        if is_first_in_row and char != R[row]:
            continue
            
        # If it's the first in col and char is not C[col], invalid
        if is_first_in_col and char != C[col]:
            continue
            
        # Place the character
        grid[row][col] = char
        
        # Continue
        if backtrack(row, col + 1):
            return True
            
        # Backtrack
        grid[row][col] = '.'
        
    return False

if backtrack(0, 0):
    print("Yes")
    for i in range(N):
        print(''.join(grid[i]))
else:
    print("No")
```

N = int(input())
R = input().strip()
C = input().strip()

# Initialize grid with empty cells
grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]

def check_constraints():
    # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for i in range(N):
        row_chars = []
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                row_chars.append(grid[i][j])
        if len(row_chars) != 3:
            return False
        if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
            return False
    
    # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    for j in range(N):
        col_chars = []
        for i in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                col_chars.append(grid[i][j])
        if len(col_chars) != 3:
            return False
        if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
            return False
    
    # Check leftmost constraints
    for i in range(N):
        leftmost_col = -1
        for j in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                if leftmost_col == -1:
                    leftmost_col = j
                break
        if leftmost_col != -1 and grid[i][leftmost_col] != R[i]:
            return False
    
    # Check topmost constraints
    for j in range(N):
        topmost_row = -1
        for i in range(N):
            if grid[i][j] != '.':
                if topmost_row == -1:
                    topmost_row = i
                break
        if topmost_row != -1 and grid[topmost_row][j] != C[j]:
            return False
    
    return True

def backtrack(row, col):
    if row == N:
        return check_constraints()
    
    if col == N:
        return backtrack(row + 1, 0)
    
    # Try empty cell
    if backtrack(row, col + 1):
        return True
    
    # Try A, B, C
    for char in ['A', 'B', 'C']:
        # Check if placing char at (row, col) would violate the constraints
        # Check if this placement makes the leftmost cell invalid
        is_first_in_row = True
        for j in range(col):
            if grid[row][j] != '.':
                is_first_in_row = False
                break
        
        # Check if this placement makes the topmost cell invalid
        is_first_in_col = True
        for i in range(row):
            if grid[i][col] != '.':
                is_first_in_col = False
                break
        
        # If it's the first in row and char is not R[row], invalid
        if is_first_in_row and char != R[row]:
            continue
            
        # If it's the first in col and char is not C[col], invalid
        if is_first_in_col and char != C[col]:
            continue
            
        # Place the character
        grid[row][col] = char
        
        # Continue
        if backtrack(row, col + 1):
            return True
            
        # Backtrack
        grid[row][col] = '.'
        
    return False

if backtrack(0, 0):
    print("Yes")
    for i in range(N):
        print(''.join(grid[i]))
else:
    print("No")

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_1

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

Sample Input 1

5

ABCBC

ACAAB

Sample Output 1

Yes

BA.C.

..CBA

- Each row contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- Each column contains exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost characters written in the rows are A, B, C, B, C from top to bottom.
- The topmost characters written in the columns are A, C, A, A, B from left to right.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where we need to place A, B, C characters
2. Each row and each column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C
3. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i]
4. The topmost character in column i must match C[i]
5. Empty cells are represented by '.'

## Key Insights

1. Since each row and column must have exactly one A, one B, and one C, that means each row and column has exactly 3 non-empty cells.

2. The leftmost character constraint means that in row i, the first non-empty cell must have the character R[i].

3. The topmost character constraint means that in column i, the first non-empty cell must have the character C[i].

4. We need to assign positions for A, B, C such that all constraints are satisfied.

## Approach

Since N is small (3 ≤ N ≤ 5), we can use backtracking or try to construct a solution systematically.

Let me think about the constraints:
- Each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
- Each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
- The leftmost character in row i is R[i]
- The topmost character in column i is C[i]

The leftmost character constraint tells us where each row's first non-empty cell is. Similarly, the topmost constraint tells us where each column's first non-empty cell is.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. First, check if the constraints are even possible:
   - For each row, R[i] must be one of A, B, C (given)
   - For each column, C[i] must be one of A, B, C (given)
   - We need to place exactly 3 characters per row and column

2. For each row i, we know the leftmost character is R[i]. This means the first non-empty cell in row i must have character R[i].

3. For each column i, we know the topmost character is C[i]. This means the first non-empty cell in column i must have character C[i].

4. We need to assign positions for the remaining characters (A, B, C) such that each row and column gets exactly one of each.

5. We can use backtracking to try all valid assignments, or we can construct a solution directly.

Actually, since N is small, we can try to construct a solution by:

1. First, place the leftmost characters as required (for row constraints)
2. Then, ensure the topmost characters as required (for column constraints)
3. Finally, place the remaining A, B, C characters to satisfy the one-per-row/column requirement

But we need to be careful about conflicts.

Let me implement a backtracking solution that tries to assign positions:

```python
    # After some thought, I realize that a simpler approach might work:
    # Since N is small (3-5), and we have the leftmost and topmost constraints, 
    # we can try to find a valid assignment using backtracking with pruning.
    
    # Let's implement a clean backtracking solution
    
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid_grid():
        # Check row constraints
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
            # Check leftmost constraint
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check column constraints
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:

    def backtrack(row):
        if row == N:
            return is_valid_grid()
        
        # Try all possible positions for A, B, C in this row
        # We need to choose 3 distinct columns
        from itertools import combinations, permutations
        
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if the leftmost position in this row has the required character
            leftmost_col = min(cols)
            # We need to place R[row] at the leftmost position
            # So we need to assign R[row] to one of the positions in cols, and it must be at leftmost_col
            for chars in permutations(['A', 'B', 'C']):
                # Create a copy of current grid
                temp_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                
                # Place the characters
                for col_idx, char in zip(cols, chars):
                    temp_grid[row][col_idx] = char
                
                # Check if leftmost position has R[row]
                leftmost_actual = -1
                for j in range(N):
                    if temp_grid[row][j] != '.':
                        leftmost_actual = j
                        break
                if leftmost_actual == -1:
                    continue
                if temp_grid[row][leftmost_actual] != R[row]:
                    continue
                
                # If we reach here, this placement is valid for this row
                # Recurse to next row
                grid = [row[:] for row in temp_grid]
                if backtrack(row + 1):
                    return True
                    
        return False
    
    # Try to find a solution
    if backtrack(0):
```

Wait, I see a problem with my approach. The backtracking is trying to assign positions for each row independently, but it's not considering that the same column can't have multiple characters. Let me fix this.

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # We'll use backtracking to assign positions for A, B, C
    # For each row, we need to choose 3 positions and assign A, B, C to them
    # with the constraint that the leftmost character is R[i]
    # and for each column, the topmost character is C[j]
    
    # Create initial grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid():
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
            # Check leftmost constraint
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
            # Check topmost constraint
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row):
        if row == N:
            return is_valid()
            
        # Try all combinations of 3 positions for this row
        from itertools import combinations, permutations
        
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if placing A,B,C at these positions satisfies the leftmost constraint
            leftmost_pos = min(cols)
            
            # Try all permutations of A,B,C
            for chars in permutations(['A', 'B', 'C']):
                # Create a temporary grid for this row
                temp_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                
                # Place the characters in this row
                for col_idx, char in zip(cols, chars):
                    temp_grid[row][col_idx] = char
                    
                # Check if leftmost position has the required character R[row]
                leftmost_actual = -1
                for j in range(N):
                    if temp_grid[row][j] != '.':
                        leftmost_actual = j
                        break
                if leftmost_actual == -1:
                    continue
                if temp_grid[row][leftmost_actual] != R[row]:
                    continue
                    
                # If we reach here, this placement is valid for this row
                # Try this placement and recurse
                grid = [row[:] for row in temp_grid]
                if backtrack(row + 1):
                    return True
                    
        return False
    
    if backtrack(0):
        print("Yes")
        for row in grid:
            print(''.join(row))
    else:
        print("No")
```

def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # We'll use backtracking to assign positions for A, B, C
    # For each row, we need to choose 3 positions and assign A, B, C to them
    # with the constraint that the leftmost character is R[i]
    # and for each column, the topmost character is C[j]
    
    # Create initial grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    def is_valid():
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if row_chars.count('A') != 1 or row_chars.count('B') != 1 or row_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
            # Check leftmost constraint
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
                
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3:
                return False
            if col_chars.count('A') != 1 or col_chars.count('B') != 1 or col_chars.count('C') != 1:
                return False
                
            # Check topmost constraint
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
                
        return True
    
    def backtrack(row):
        if row == N:
            return is_valid()
            
        # Try all combinations of 3 positions for this row
        from itertools import combinations, permutations
        
        for cols in combinations(range(N), 3):
            # Check if placing A,B,C at these positions satisfies the leftmost constraint
            leftmost_pos = min(cols)
            
            # Try all permutations of A,B,C
            for chars in permutations(['A', 'B', 'C']):
                # Create a temporary grid for this row
                temp_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                
                # Place the characters in this row
                for col_idx, char in zip(cols, chars):
                    temp_grid[row][col_idx] = char
                    
                # Check if leftmost position has the required character R[row]
                leftmost_actual = -1

                        leftmost_actual = j
                        break

                    continue

                    continue

        return False

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given an integer N and strings R and C of length N consisting of A, B, and C. Solve the following problem.
There is a N \times N grid. All cells are initially empty.

You can write at most one character from A, B, and C in each cell. (You can also leave the cell empty.)
Determine if it is possible to satisfy all of the following conditions, and if it is possible, print one way to do so.

- Each row and each column contain exactly one A, one B, and one C.
- The leftmost character written in the i-th row matches the i-th character of R.
- The topmost character written in the i-th column matches the i-th character of C.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N

R

C

Output

If there is no way to fill the grid to satisfy the conditions in the problem statement, print No in one line.

Otherwise, print one such way to fill the grid in the following format:
Yes

A_2

\vdots

A_N


The first line should contain Yes.

The i-th of the subsequent N lines should contain a string A_i of length N.

- If the j-th character of A_i is ., it indicates that the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left is empty.
- If the j-th character of A_i is A, it indicates that A is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is B, it indicates that B is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.
- If the j-th character of A_i is C, it indicates that C is written in the cell in the i-th row from the top and the j-th column from the left.

If there are multiple correct ways to fill the grid, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- N is an integer between 3 and 5, inclusive.
- R and C are strings of length N consisting of A, B, and C.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an N×N grid where we need to place exactly one A, one B, and one C in each row and each column.
2. The leftmost character in row i must match R[i].
3. The topmost character in column i must match C[i].
4. We need to determine if such a configuration is possible.

## Key Insights

1. Since each row and column must contain exactly one A, one B, and one C, we need to place these characters appropriately.
2. The leftmost character constraint means that in each row, the first non-empty cell must match R[i].
3. The topmost character constraint means that in each column, the first non-empty cell must match C[i].
4. For the constraints to be satisfiable:
   - Each row needs to start with the specified character (R[i])
   - Each column needs to start with the specified character (C[i])
   - We must be able to place exactly one A, B, C in each row and column

## Approach

1. First, check if the constraints are even possible:
   - For a row to start with R[i], the first non-empty cell must be that character
   - For a column to start with C[i], the first non-empty cell must be that character
   - We need to ensure that we can satisfy both constraints

2. Since N is small (3-5), we can use backtracking or try to construct a valid solution directly.

3. Strategy:
   - Place the required characters based on the row and column constraints
   - For each row i, the first character must be R[i]
   - For each column j, the first character must be C[j]
   - We need to find positions for A, B, C such that each row and column has exactly one of each

4. Since we have exactly one of each character per row and column, we can think of this as a permutation problem with constraints.

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Initialize grid with empty cells
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    
    # First, place the required characters based on row and column constraints
    # For each row i, the first non-empty character must be R[i]
    # For each column j, the first non-empty character must be C[j]
    
    # We need to find positions where we place A, B, C
    # Each row must have exactly one A, one B, one C
    # Each column must have exactly one A, one B, one C
    
    # Try to construct a solution by placing characters according to constraints
    
    # First, check if constraints are compatible
    # For the first row, the leftmost character is R[0], so grid[0][0] should be R[0]
    # For the first column, the topmost character is C[0], so grid[0][0] should be C[0]
    # Therefore, R[0] must equal C[0]
    
    # Actually, let's check if it's possible to satisfy both constraints
    # For each position (i,j), if (i,j) is the first non-empty in row i, it must be R[i]
    # If (i,j) is the first non-empty in column j, it must be C[j]
    
    # So for any cell that is the first non-empty in its row and column, we need R[i] == C[j]
    
    # Let's try to place the characters
    # We know that in row i, the leftmost non-empty character is R[i]
    # We know that in column j, the topmost non-empty character is C[j]
    
    # The key insight: the cell at (i,j) could be the first non-empty in both row i and column j
    # In that case, we need R[i] == C[j]
    
    # So for all i,j, if a cell (i,j) is the first non-empty in both row i and column j,
    # then R[i] must equal C[j]
    
    # However, there might be multiple possibilities. Let's use backtracking.
    
    # But since N is small (3-5), we can try to construct a solution
    # We know we need exactly one A, B, C per row and column
    
    # Let's try to place characters systematically
    
    # First, check if R[0] == C[0]. This is necessary because cell (0,0) would be first in both row 0 and column 0
    # If not, it's impossible
    
    # However, the first non-empty cell in a row might not be at (i,0). It could be at (i,j) for any j.
    # But if in row i, the first non-empty is at column j, then for column j, the first non-empty must be at row i (or above).
    
    # This is getting complex. Let's try a different approach.
    
    # Since N is small, we can try all possible positions for the A, B, C in each row and column,
    # ensuring the constraints are met.
    
    # For each row, we need to place A, B, C in three different columns
    # For each column, we need to place A, B, C in three different rows
    
    # We can use backtracking to assign positions
    
    def is_valid_placement(grid):
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(row_chars) != 3 or len(set(row_chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(col_chars) != 3 or len(set(col_chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost characters in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False  # No character in row i
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost characters in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False  # No character in column j
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    # Try to construct a valid placement
    # We'll use backtracking to place A, B, C in positions
    # For each row, we need to pick 3 columns for A, B, C
    # For each column, we need to pick 3 rows for A, B, C
    
    # Since constraints are small, let's try to build it directly
    
    # First, ensure that if a cell (i,j) is the leftmost in row i and topmost in column j,
    # then R[i] must equal C[j]
    # But actually, leftmost in row i might not be at column j, and topmost in column j might not be at row i
    
    # Alternative approach: try to place the characters such that:
    # For row i, the first non-empty character (leftmost) is at position j where j is the first column with a non-empty cell
    # For column j, the first non-empty character (topmost) is at position i where i is the first row with a non-empty cell
    
    # This is complex. Let's try to use the fact that each row must have exactly one of each letter
    
    # We can try to assign positions for A, B, C in each row such that:
    # 1. The leftmost position has the character R[i]
    # 2. Each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
    
    from itertools import permutations
    
    # Try all possible ways to assign columns to A, B, C in each row
    # For each row, we need to assign a permutation of 3 columns
    
    # First, check if it's even possible based on the leftmost constraints
    
    # For row i, if R[i] is A, then the first non-empty cell must have A
    # For row i, if R[i] is B, then the first non-empty cell must have B
    # For row i, if R[i] is C, then the first non-empty cell must have C
    
    # The first non-empty cell in row i must be the first column where we place a character
    
    # So for row i, the leftmost column where we place a character must have the character R[i]
    
    # Similarly, for column j, the topmost row where we place a character must have the character C[j]
    
    # So we need to assign for each row i: a set of 3 columns where we place A, B, C
    # with the constraint that the minimum column index has the character R[i]
    
    # And for each column j: a set of 3 rows where we place A, B, C
    # with the constraint that the minimum row index has the character C[j]
    
    # This is a constraint satisfaction problem.
    
    # Since N is small (3-5), we can try all possible assignments.
    
    def backtrack(row, grid, used_cols):
        if row == N:
            # Check if the grid satisfies all constraints
            return is_valid_placement(grid)
        
        # For current row, try all possible positions for A, B, C
        # But we need to ensure the leftmost character is R[row]
        
        # First, find all possible column assignments for this row
        # We need to choose 3 columns out of N for A, B, C
        # And the leftmost column must have character R[row]
        
        # Try each possible permutation of columns
        from itertools import permutations
        cols = list(range(N))
        
        for perm in permutations(cols):
            # Check if this permutation satisfies the leftmost constraint
            # The leftmost column is perm[0]
            if R[row] != grid[row][perm[0]]:
                # We can't just assign, we need to maintain the grid
                pass
            
            # Instead, let's build the grid step by step
            pass
    
    # Simpler approach: try all possible ways to assign positions
    
    # Since N is small, we can use brute force to try all valid configurations
    
    # But there are too many possibilities. Let's think of constraints.
    
    # Observation: the cell (i,j) can only be the leftmost in row i if it's the first non-empty cell in that row
    # and it can only be the topmost in column j if it's the first non-empty cell in that column
    
    # For the constraints to be satisfied, if cell (i,j) is both the leftmost in row i and topmost in column j,
    # then R[i] must equal C[j]
    
    # So for all i,j, if (i,j) is a cell that is the first in both row i and column j,
    # then R[i] == C[j]
    
    # We can try to build the solution by placing characters in a way that satisfies this.
    
    # Actually, let's use a known fact: in any valid configuration, for each row i,
    # the leftmost non-empty cell is at column p_i where p_i is the first column with a non-empty cell,
    # and grid[i][p_i] = R[i].
    
    # Similarly, for each column j, the topmost non-empty cell is at row q_j where q_j is the first row with a non-empty cell,
    # and grid[q_j][j] = C[j].
    
    # Now, if the leftmost in row i is at column p_i and the topmost in column p_i is at row q_{p_i},
    # then we must have grid[q_{p_i} ][p_i] = R[i] and grid[q_{p_i} ][p_i] = C[p_i].
    # Therefore, R[i] = C[p_i].
    
    # This gives us a constraint: for each row i, if the leftmost column is p_i, then R[i] = C[p_i].
    
    # Similarly, if the topmost in column j is at row q_j, then C[j] = R[q_j].
    
    # So we have: R[i] = C[p_i] for the leftmost column p_i of row i
    # And C[j] = R[q_j] for the topmost row q_j of column j
    
    # Therefore, for any i, if we know the leftmost column p_i of row i, we must have R[i] = C[p_i]
    
    # This gives us a necessary condition. Also, each column j must have a topmost row q_j where C[j] = R[q_j]
    
    # So the necessary condition is that for every position where a row has leftmost column j, we need R[i] = C[j]
    
    # But we don't know which column is the leftmost for each row.
    
    # Let's try to see if it's possible by checking all possible leftmost column assignments.
    
    # For each row i, the leftmost column must be such that R[i] = C[j] for that column j.
    
    # This means: for row i, the leftmost column j must satisfy C[j] == R[i]
    
    # So row i can only have its leftmost character in columns j where C[j] == R[i]
    
    # Similarly, for column j, the topmost row i must satisfy R[i] == C[j] (since C[j] = R[i])
    
    # So column j can only have its topmost character in rows i where R[i] == C[j]
    
    # Now, we can proceed:
    # 1. For each row i, determine which columns j can be its leftmost column: those where C[j] == R[i]
    # 2. For each column j, determine which rows i can be its topmost row: those where R[i] == C[j]
    
    # We need to assign leftmost columns to rows such that:
    # - For row i, leftmost column j satisfies C[j] == R[i]
    # - For column j, topmost row i satisfies R[i] == C[j]
    # - Each row has exactly one leftmost column (the first non-empty cell)
    # - Each column has exactly one topmost row (the first non-empty cell)
    
    # This is a bipartite matching problem where:
    # - Left side: rows 0 to N-1
    # - Right side: columns 0 to N-1
    # - Edge between row i and column j exists if C[j] == R[i]
    
    # But we also need to ensure that in the final configuration, each row and column has exactly one A, B, C.
    
    # Actually, since each row must have exactly one A, B, C, and the leftmost is already constrained,
    # we need to assign the other two characters to other columns.
    
    # And each column must have exactly one A, B, C, with the topmost already constrained.
    
    # So the question reduces to: can we assign leftmost columns to rows such that:
    # 1. For each row i, the leftmost column j has C[j] == R[i]
    # 2. For each column j, the topmost row i has R[i] == C[j]
    # 3. The assignments are consistent
    
    # Actually, condition 2 is automatically satisfied if condition 1 is satisfied and the topmost row is determined by the assignment.
    
    # But we need to ensure that the topmost row for column j is the same as the row that has j as its leftmost column.
    
    # Wait, the topmost row for column j is the first row that has a non-empty cell in column j.
    # This could be different from the row that has j as its leftmost column.
    
    # For example, row i might have its leftmost at column j, but column j might have its topmost at row k where k < i.
    
    # So we need to ensure that if row i has its leftmost at column j, then row k < i cannot have a non-empty cell in column j.
    
    # This is getting very complex. Let's try a simpler approach with small N.
    
    # Since N is only 3-5, we can try all possible ways to assign the leftmost columns.
    
    # For each row i, leftmost column must be one of the columns j where C[j] == R[i]
    
    # We need to assign a leftmost column to each row such that:
    # 1. For each row i, the assigned column j has C[j] == R[i]
    # 2. For each column j, if it is assigned as leftmost to row i, then no row k < i has a non-empty cell in column j
    # 3. We can also have the constraint that for column j, the topmost row must have R[topmost_row] == C[j]
    
    # But the topmost row for column j might be any row where there's a non-empty cell in column j, not necessarily the row that assigned j as leftmost.
    
    # Actually, if a column j has a non-empty cell at row k, and k is the smallest such k, then the topmost is at row k, and we need R[k] == C[j].
    
    # So for any column j that has a non-empty cell, the topmost row k must satisfy R[k] == C[j].
    
    # This gives us a constraint: in any valid configuration, for each column j, if it has a non-empty cell at row k, then R[k] == C[j].
    
    # Similarly, for each row i, if it has a non-empty cell at column j, then if j is the leftmost, we have R[i] == C[j].
    
    # But if j is not the leftmost, we still have the character constraint: the character in (i,j) is A, B, or C, but it might not be R[i] or C[j].
    
    # However, the leftmost character constraint only applies to the first non-empty cell.
    
    # The character in a cell is determined by what we place in it.
    
    # I think I'm overcomplicating. Let's go back to the sample.
    
    # Sample 1: N=5, R="ABCBC", C="ACAAB"
    # Row 0: leftmost must be A, so first non-empty must be A
    # Row 1: leftmost must be B, so first non-empty must be B
    # Row 2: leftmost must be C, so first non-empty must be C
    # Row 3: leftmost must be B, so first non-empty must be B
    # Row 4: leftmost must be C, so first non-empty must be C
    
    # For columns:
    # Col 0: topmost must be A, so first non-empty must be A
    # Col 1: topmost must be C, so first non-empty must be C
    # Col 2: topmost must be A, so first non-empty must be A
    # Col 3: topmost must be A, so first non-empty must be A
    # Col 4: topmost must be B, so first non-empty must be B
    
    # So cell (0,0): if it's the first non-empty in both row 0 and col 0, then R[0]=A, C[0]=A, so A is possible.
    # Cell (1,1): if it's first in row 1 and col 1, then R[1]=B, C[1]=C, so B≠C, impossible.
    
    # In the sample output, row 0: "AC..B" - so first non-empty is at col 0: 'A', which matches R[0]='A'
    # Row 1: ".BA.C" - first non-empty at col 1: 'B', which matches R[1]='B'
    # Row 2: "C.BA." - first non-empty at col 0: 'C', which matches R[2]='C'
    # Row 3: "BA.C." - first non-empty at col 0: 'B', which matches R[3]='B'
    # Row 4: "..CBA" - first non-empty at col 2: 'C', which matches R[4]='C'
    
    # For columns:
    # Col 0: first non-empty at row 0: 'A', which matches C[0]='A'
    # Col 1: first non-empty at row 1: 'B', which matches C[1]='C'? No! C[1]='C' but we have 'B' at (1,1)
    
    # Wait, in row 1, col 1 has 'B', so is that the first non-empty in col 1?
    # Let's check col 1: positions: (0,1)='C', (1,1)='B', (2,1)='.' , (3,1)='A', (4,1)='B'
    # The first non-empty is at (0,1)='C', which matches C[1]='C' ✓
    
    # In row 0, col 1 has 'C', which is not empty, so col 1 has first non-empty at (0,1)='C'.
    
    # So in general, for column j, the first non-empty cell is at the smallest i such that grid[i][j] != '.'.
    
    # And we need grid[i][j] == C[j].
    
    # So the algorithm:
    # 1. Find for each row i, the column j where the leftmost non-empty cell occurs.
    # 2. For each column j, find the row i where the topmost non-empty cell occurs.
    # 3. For the cell at (i,j) to be the leftmost in row i and topmost in column j, we need R[i] == C[j].
    # 4. But it might be that the leftmost in row i is at j, and the topmost in column j is at k, with k < i.
    
    # So if the topmost in column j is at row k < i, then there is a non-empty cell in (k,j) with value C[j].
    # And if the leftmost in row i is at j, then there is a non-empty cell in (i,j) with value R[i].
    
    # This is consistent as long as at the topmost row k for column j, we have R[k] == C[j].
    
    # And at the leftmost row i for row i, we have R[i] == C[j] if it's the first in both.
    
    # Actually, for any cell (i,j) that is the first in its row and column, we need R[i] == C[j].
    
    # But a cell might be the first in its row but not in its column, or vice versa.
    
    # However, if a cell is the first in its row and also in its column, then we need R[i] == C[j].
    
    # So we can try to assign the positions.
    
    # Since N is small, we can try all possible ways to assign the leftmost columns to rows.
    
    # For each row i, the leftmost column j must satisfy that in column j, the first non-empty cell occurs at some row k, and we need R[i] == C[j] only if (i,j) is the first in both.
    
    # This is too complex. Let's try a different approach: since the grid is small, we can try to construct a solution using backtracking.
    
    # We'll try to assign for each row, the positions of A, B, C.
    
    def build_solution():
        # Try to find a valid assignment
        # We'll use backtracking to assign positions for A, B, C in each row
        # and ensure the column constraints are met
        
        from itertools import permutations
        
        # Generate all possible column assignments for a row
        # For row i, we need to choose 3 distinct columns out of N
        # and the leftmost of these 3 columns must have the character R[i]
        
        def is_valid_placement(grid):
            # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
            for i in range(N):
                row_chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
                if len(row_chars) != 3 or len(set(row_chars)) != 3:
                    return False
            
            # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
            for j in range(N):
                col_chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
                if len(col_chars) != 3 or len(set(col_chars)) != 3:
                    return False
            
            # Check leftmost characters in each row
            for i in range(N):
                leftmost = -1
                for j in range(N):
                    if grid[i][j] != '.':
                        leftmost = j
                        break
                if leftmost == -1:
                    return False
                if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                    return False
            
            # Check topmost characters in each column
            for j in range(N):
                topmost = -1
                for i in range(N):
                    if grid[i][j] != '.':
                        topmost = i
                        break
                if topmost == -1:
                    return False
                if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                    return False
            
            return True
        
        # Try all possible ways to assign columns to A, B, C in each row
        # For each row, we need to choose 3 distinct columns and assign A, B, C to them
        # with the constraint that the leftmost column has the character R[i]
        
        from itertools import permutations
        
        def solve_for_row(row, grid, assigned_cols):
            if row == N:
                return grid if is_valid_placement(grid) else None
            
            # For current row, try all possible permutations of 3 columns out of N
            from itertools import combinations
            cols = list(range(N))
            for selected_cols in combinations(cols, 3):
                # Try all permutations of (A,B,C) for these columns
                for perm in permutations(['A','B','C']):
                    # Create a new grid from current grid
                    new_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                    
                    # Place the characters
                    for col_idx, char in zip(selected_cols, perm):
                        new_grid[row][col_idx] = char
                    
                    # Check if leftmost is R[row]
                    leftmost = -1
                    for j in range(N):
                        if new_grid[row][j] != '.':
                            leftmost = j
                            break
                    if leftmost == -1:
                        continue
                    if new_grid[row][leftmost] != R[row]:
                        continue
                    
                    # Recurse
                    result = solve_for_row(row + 1, new_grid, assigned_cols + [selected_cols])
                    if result is not None:
                        return result
            
            return None
        
        # Start with empty grid
        grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
        result = solve_for_row(0, grid, [])
        
        return result
    
    # Try to find a solution
    solution = build_solution()
    
    if solution is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in solution:
            print(''.join(row))

solve()
```

Wait, this backtracking might be too slow even for N=3, and there are many combinations. Let me optimize and simplify.

Actually, since N is at most 5, and we only need one valid solution, and the constraints are tight, I can use a more direct approach.

But let me first check if there's a necessary condition: for each row i, the leftmost non-empty cell must be R[i], and for each column j, the topmost non-empty cell must be C[j].

Also, if a cell (i,j) is both the leftmost in row i and the topmost in column j, then we must have R[i] == C[j].

In any valid configuration, for each row i, the leftmost column is some j, and for that cell (i,j), since it's the leftmost in row i, we have the character R[i].

For that cell to be the topmost in column j, we need C[j] == R[i].

But it might not be the topmost in column j - there might be a row k < i with a non-empty cell in column j.

In that case, the topmost in column j is at row k, with character C[j].

So for row k, the leftmost in row k could be at any column, but the character in (k,j) is C[j].

The key insight is that for any cell that is the first non-empty in its row and column, we need R[i] == C[j].

For cells that are first in their row but not in their column, or first in their column but not in their row, the character constraint is only on the row or column respectively.

But for the characters in a row, we need exactly one A, one B, one C.

Given the small constraints, I'll implement a backtracking solution that is more efficient.

```python
def solve():
    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Try to find a valid placement
    from itertools import permutations, combinations
    
    # Generate all possible ways to assign positions for A, B, C in each row
    # We'll use backtracking to build the grid
    
    def is_valid(grid):
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(chars) != 3 or len(set(chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(chars) != 3 or len(set(chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    # Try all possible assignments
    # For each row, try all possible ways to assign A, B, C to 3 different columns
    def backtrack(row, grid):
        if row == N:
            return grid if is_valid(grid) else None
        
        # For current row, try all combinations of 3 columns
        from itertools import combinations
        cols = list(range(N))
        for selected_cols in combinations(cols, 3):
            # Try all permutations of A, B, C for these columns
            for chars in permutations(['A', 'B', 'C']):
                # Create a copy of grid
                new_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                
                # Place the characters
                for col_idx, char in zip(selected_cols, chars):
                    new_grid[row][col_idx] = char
                
                # Check if leftmost is R[row]
                leftmost = -1
                for j in range(N):
                    if new_grid[row][j] != '.':
                        leftmost = j
                        break
                if leftmost == -1 or new_grid[row][leftmost] != R[row]:
                    continue
                
                # Recurse
                result = backtrack(row + 1, new_grid)
                if result is not None:
                    return result
        
        return None
    
    # Start with empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    result = backtrack(0, grid)
    
    if result is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result:
            print(''.join(row))

solve()
```

    N = int(input())
    R = input().strip()
    C = input().strip()
    
    # Try to find a valid placement
    from itertools import permutations, combinations
    
    # Generate all possible ways to assign positions for A, B, C in each row
    # We'll use backtracking to build the grid
    
    def is_valid(grid):
        # Check each row has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for i in range(N):
            chars = [grid[i][j] for j in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(chars) != 3 or len(set(chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check each column has exactly one A, one B, one C
        for j in range(N):
            chars = [grid[i][j] for i in range(N) if grid[i][j] != '.']
            if len(chars) != 3 or len(set(chars)) != 3:
                return False
        
        # Check leftmost in each row
        for i in range(N):
            leftmost = -1
            for j in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    leftmost = j
                    break
            if leftmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[i][leftmost] != R[i]:
                return False
        
        # Check topmost in each column
        for j in range(N):
            topmost = -1
            for i in range(N):
                if grid[i][j] != '.':
                    topmost = i
                    break
            if topmost == -1:
                return False
            if grid[topmost][j] != C[j]:
                return False
        
        return True
    
    # Try all possible assignments
    # For each row, try all possible ways to assign A, B, C to 3 different columns
    def backtrack(row, grid):
        if row == N:
            return grid if is_valid(grid) else None
        
        # For current row, try all combinations of 3 columns
        from itertools import combinations
        cols = list(range(N))
        for selected_cols in combinations(cols, 3):
            # Try all permutations of A, B, C for these columns
            for chars in permutations(['A', 'B', 'C']):
                # Create a copy of grid
                new_grid = [row[:] for row in grid]
                
                # Place the characters
                for col_idx, char in zip(selected_cols, chars):
                    new_grid[row][col_idx] = char

                        leftmost = j
                        break

                if result is not None:
                    return result
        
        return None
    
    # Start with empty grid
    grid = [['.' for _ in range(N)] for _ in range(N)]
    result = backtrack(0, grid)
    
    if result is None:
        print("No")
    else:
        print("Yes")
        for row in result:
            print(''.join(row))

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There is a grid with H rows and W columns.

The square at the i-th row and j-th column contains a string S_{i,j} of length 5.

The rows are labeled with the numbers from 1 through H, and the columns are labeled with the uppercase English letters from `A` through the W-th letter of the alphabet.

<image>

Exactly one of the squares in the grid contains the string `snuke`. Find this square and report its location.

For example, the square at the 6-th row and 8-th column should be reported as `H6`.

Constraints

* 1≦H, W≦26
* The length of S_{i,j} is 5.
* S_{i,j} consists of lowercase English letters (`a`-`z`).
* Exactly one of the given strings is equal to `snuke`.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:


H W
S_{1,1} S_{1,2} ... S_{1,W}
S_{2,1} S_{2,2} ... S_{2,W}
:
S_{H,1} S_{H,2} ... S_{H,W}


Output

Print the labels of the row and the column of the square containing the string `snuke`, with no space inbetween.

Examples

Input

15 10
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snuke snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake

snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake
snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake snake


Output

H6
